=== 2026-09-30 03:15 UTC ===

SECOND MATERIAL CHANGE IN TWO DAYS: CHILE MOVES FROM NO TO PARTIAL.
Tally is now 16 Yes / 14 Partial / 30 No, from 16/13/31.

CHILE — AND THE REASON MATTERS AS MUCH AS THE RESULT. Yesterday I refused to reclassify this row on
the strength of a bill title, because boletín 18510-07 is called "Modifica el Código Penal en
materia de trabajo forzoso y dispone reglas sobre trazabilidad de productos" and its metadata
contains zero occurrences of "importación". Reading the text proved that refusal right, but in the
OPPOSITE direction from the one I expected. Article 2 of the bill, read at primary text:
"Prohíbase la producción, transporte, distribución, comercialización, IMPORTACIÓN y exportación de
bienes, productos o mercancías respecto de los cuales existan antecedentes suficientes para
presumir que han sido obtenidos, total o parcialmente, mediante trabajo forzoso, servidumbre,
esclavitud o cualquier otra forma de explotación laboral prohibida por la ley." It also sets a fine
of up to 100 UTM plus decomiso, requires a semiannual high-risk product list, and its considerando
names Canada's import ban as the model. The lesson is not "titles mislead toward No" but "read the
text, in either direction".

The route was new: the Chamber's open-data SOAP operation retornarDocumentoIngresoProyectoLey
returns the bill as a base64 PDF. A TOOLING FINDING WORTH KEEPING: this file's usual zlib stream
extraction recovered 601 words and ZERO hits for "importación" from that PDF, while pypdfium2
recovered 11,380 characters including the article above. Two methods on one document disagreed
completely, and the weaker one would have left this row misclassified. pypdfium2 is available here
and should be tried whenever the stream method returns an implausibly low word count.
Partial, not Yes: it is an unsponsored deputy's moción with no votes recorded and may never advance,
but a formally tabled bill naming importation is Partial under the standing rule. Two baseline
errors corrected: this file had recorded that no Chilean boletín exists, and that the measure was
criminal-code only.

COSTA RICA IS NOW THE MOST LIKELY NEXT CHANGE, and is held at No only because the text is unread.
Decreto Ejecutivo N.° 45941-H-MTSS-COMEX, signed 7 September and published in La Gaceta on
28 September, Alcance N.° 129, creates an official list of goods at risk of forced labour. The
accounts CONFLICT on the one point that decides the classification: La Nación says it establishes a
registry rather than imposing import prohibitions, while El Observador says the aim is to prohibit
and prevent entry. Both cannot be right about its legal effect. Holding at No is conservative and
explicitly provisional pending the text.

PERU: the Tuesday session convened and nothing was decided. Verified at Gestión — the commission
"paused" to Wednesday 30 September rather than voting, and after more than three and a half hours
had covered only one subject area out of a request containing 66 measures. So the predictamen is
still unvoted and the delegation still not rejected. A DANGEROUS HEADLINE TRAP is logged: Correo ran
"Comisión de Constitución concluye no dar facultades" when no such conclusion was reached — the
headline treats the technical team's draft as a decision of the commission. New event: Fuerza
Popular filed a moción de censura against chair Avendaño during the session, alleging "falta de
transparencia"; she ruled it did not proceed. The debate never reached the customs bloc.

A BASELINE GAP FOUND AND FIXED IN THE BRIEF: the standing baseline has cited only doc 2026-15181 as
the 28 July operative action. Doc 2026-15274 was published the same day and is a separate
PRESIDENTIAL DOCUMENT, "Actions by the United States in the Investigations Under Section 301 ... of
60 Economies Related to the Failure of Each Economy To Impose and Effectively Enforce a Prohibition
on the Importation of Goods Produced With Forced Labor" — verified directly at the Federal Register
API. There are two operative instruments of that date, not one.

A WAF QUESTION SETTLED AND ONE OF MY OWN ENTRIES CORRECTED. The "Request Rejected" body at
douane.gov.ma is MOROCCO'S OWN WAF, not our proxy: it arrives as an HTTP 200 with an origin-issued
support ID that differs per request, whereas our proxy's denial fails at the tunnel with a CONNECT
502. Applying the same test, customs.gov.om's circulars page — which I attributed to "our own edge"
on 27 September — is also the origin's WAF. That earlier attribution was wrong and is corrected.
Separately, diariooficial.gov.co does fail with the proxy's CONNECT 502, so Colombia's Diario
Oficial edition and entry-into-force date remain unfound and must not be inferred as 29 September.

Smaller results: Indonesia's Permendag 18/2026, an open item since 27 September, is closed — it is
an import-licensing amendment with zero "kerja paksa" content. Argentina's draft turns out to be
EX POST by design, detecting breaches after goods enter rather than blocking them, so even
publication would not automatically move that row. Norway's register is still empty on the last day
of September, which is expected, and the row returns to the live list tomorrow. New Zealand
dissolves tomorrow. Honduras is at day eleven with no October index yet.

One coverage gap to record honestly: the batch covering Peru disclosed that it read only part of its
baseline file, so Sri Lanka, South Africa and South Korea went unread there, and six of its ten
economies were not checked at all this run.

=== 2026-10-01 02:19 UTC ===
HEADLINE: Costa Rica moves No -> Partial, the third material change in three days. Tally now 16 Yes / 15 Partial / 29 No (was 16/14/30).

USTR FINAL DETERMINATION: no change. All six batches independently confirmed via the Federal Register API that the final forced-labour action remains doc 2026-15181 (published 2026-07-28, tariffs effective 2026-07-24), alongside Presidential Document 2026-15274 of 28 July. Everything USTR has published since is unrelated: 2026-16841 (China WTO, 08-18), 2026-17351 (Notorious Markets, 08-26), 2026-17820 (Russia WTO, 09-01), 2026-17925 (China tech-transfer exclusions, 09-02), 2026-18775 (NTE 2027, 09-14), 2026-19399 (sugar TRQ, 09-23). No modification, exclusion process or tier change exists. Note the recurring trigger text still describes this determination as "expected imminently"; that wording is stale and the file reflects the actual position.

COSTA RICA, No -> Partial (limb (c)). Decreto Ejecutivo N.o 45941-H-MTSS-COMEX, signed 7 September, published in La Gaceta Alcance N.o 129 of 28 September, in force on publication (Article 17). The El Observador / La Nacion conflict flagged yesterday is resolved in LA NACION's favour, and it turns on purpose clause versus operative clause. Article 1 (Objeto) does speak of preventing goods made with forced labour from entering the national territory, which read alone sounds like a ban and is evidently what El Observador read. But the only operative border provision, Article 12, says the competent authority "adoptara las medidas de seguimiento y control correspondientes" -- monitoring and control, not refusal of entry. Article 4's prohibition is of forced labour "en el territorio nacional", i.e. domestic conduct, not importation. Article 13 imposes a due-diligence duty satisfied merely by checking a list. The decree's own title is "Medidas relativas a la prohibicion del trabajo forzoso" -- prohibition of forced LABOUR, not of imports. The regime is also not self-executing: it keys off a Lista Oficial that does not yet exist, due within 60 natural days (Transitorio Unico). Enacted and published, so not limb (a); a real but incomplete regime, so Partial under limb (c), the same limb as the United Kingdom. Verified twice: a research pass retrieved La Gaceta Alcance 129 from the Imprenta Nacional (HTTP 200, 6.2 MB, 244 pages via pypdfium2; pgrweb.go.cr returned 403), and the operative articles were then re-read independently here before the row was moved.

HONDURAS: two new gazette editions and the pagination rule paid off again. 37,258 (29 Sept) and 37,259 (30 Sept) appeared on index page two, beyond the 37,256 ceiling. Both downloaded and extracted with pypdfium2 at good quality; neither contains "PCM-014", "Anexo I" or "trabajo forzoso". 37,258's two "forzos" hits are the catalogued confusable "reduccion forzosa de personal". EDITION 37,257 IS RECORDED AS OUTSTANDING, NOT AS A NUMBERING SKIP -- the discipline learned from the 37,241 error. All index offsets (0/12/24/36) were checked. October index still 404, now correctly read as "not yet created" rather than a route failure.

PERU: the Wednesday 30 September session came and went WITHOUT A VOTE, and the shape of the dispute changed. The Correo trap recurred in a new form -- a 30/09 07:00 piece calling a vote "probable" -- and was again declined as a forecast, not an outcome.

A draft being prepared behind the scenes is not a formally tabled measure and does not reach limb (a). If it is tabled, Switzerland moves to Partial -- that is now the specific trigger to watch.

Informative that a national newspaper made exactly the conflation this row guards against, twice.

PERU -- THE VOTE FINALLY HAPPENED, AFTER FOUR RUNS OF WAITING, AND IT IS BAD NEWS FOR THE DELEGATION ROUTE. On Thursday 1 October the Comision de Constitucion ADOPTED a merged multipartidario texto sustitutorio and did NOT adopt the predictamen to archive. The delegation survives but is cut to TWO materias -- citizen security and the Fenomeno El Nino -- for 90 days instead of 120, reportedly 18 points extracted from the original 66, drafted by the technical teams of Renovacion Popular, Civico Obras, Ahora Nacion and Buen Gobierno. CONSEQUENCE FOR THIS TRACKER: Civico Obras's "facilitacion del comercio" item -- the customs bloc this row turned on -- survives in no stated form, so a Decreto Legislativo carrying a forced-labour import ban now looks UNLIKELY through delegation, and the July bill reverts to being the only plausible vehicle. HONDURAS -- EDITION 37,257 FOUND, SEPTEMBER COMPLETE, AND A CLAIM FROM YESTERDAY WITHDRAWN. 37,257 sits ALONE at ?start=24 of the September index (slug 20260928-37257, viewdocument/4911). It was downloaded and read here directly -- 3,395,479 bytes, 32 pages, 8,969 words via pypdfium2 -- and is CLEAN, with zero hits for forzos, forzad, trabajo forzoso, PCM-014, Anexo I, SETRASS, prohibici, import and Lista de Mercanc; its three "aduana" hits are one customs personnel lawsuit. THE WITHDRAWAL: yesterday this file recorded that index offsets above 12 "repeat page two". That was WRONG, and the mechanism is a NEW failure mode worth keeping: yesterday the index held 24 entries, so ?start=24 was OUT OF RANGE and the site silently served the last populated page -- indistinguishable from a repeated page. Today a 25th entry exists and ?start=24 returns it alone. The only safe method is to page until the returned set stops changing, then re-check after any new entry. A URL-form hypothesis (/index.php prefix) was tested and REJECTED. Note 37,257 is DATED 28 September yet was posted after 1 October 02:22 UTC, so the index is not strictly date-ordered and late posting is confirmed as real -- vindicating the standing rule that a missing edition is "not yet found", never a numbering skip, for the second time. September now reads 37,235-37,259 with no gaps, all clean. The October index flipped from 404 to HTTP 200 today but carries zero entries at offsets 0, 12 and 24.

INDONESIA -- TREATY CLAUSE NOW VERIFIED FROM THE PRIMARY TEXT. The US-Indonesia ART PDF was downloaded from ustr.gov and extracted (45 pages). The clause is genuinely at ARTICLE 2.9 ("Labor") paragraph 1 -- the snippet-sourced number held up, though it was rightly treated as unverified until the text was opened, since numbering has varied 2.9/3.9 across these agreements. It obliges Indonesia to "adopt and implement a prohibition on the importation of goods mined, produced, or manufactured wholly or in part by forced or compulsory labor", with a two-year clock from entry into force. Cumulative, not constitutive: Indonesia is already Yes on Permendag 9/2026. It does mean Indonesia also sits on the RTA-commitment pathway.

=== 2026-10-03 02:19 UTC ===
NO CLASSIFICATION CHANGES. Tally unchanged at 16 Yes / 15 Partial / 29 No. The value of this run is elsewhere: THIS FILE RETRACTED TWO OF ITS OWN CLAIMS, closed three long-standing gaps at primary-source level, and finally diagnosed four access failures it had been guessing about.

SCOPE NOTE: four of six batches had reported when this entry was written. Batches 5 (Pakistan, Peru, Philippines, Qatar, Russia, Saudi Arabia, Singapore, South Africa, South Korea, Sri Lanka) and 6 (Switzerland, Taiwan, Thailand, Trinidad and Tobago, Turkiye, UAE, United Kingdom, Uruguay, Venezuela, Vietnam) were still running, so those twenty rows carry forward unchecked in this commit. USTR: no change. Four batches independently confirmed the final action remains doc 2026-15181 (published 2026-07-28, effective 2026-07-24); the newest USTR item is still 2026-19399 (sugar TRQ, 23 Sept), and a full-text Federal Register search for "forced labor section 301" published since 24 Sept returned only an unrelated NHTSA rule. The recurring trigger text still calls the determination "expected imminently" -- still stale.

TWO RETRACTIONS, BOTH OF CLAIMS THIS FILE HAD ASSERTED:
(1) INDONESIA -- "SEVEN WORKING DAYS" WAS WRONGLY REJECTED. This row had tested that figure, failed to corroborate it in search, and recorded that it "stays REJECTED rather than merely unverified". That was wrong. The Permendag 9/2026 PDF was fetched from the ministry's own JDIH portal and read; Article 4 states verbatim "Importir dapat menyampaikan klarifikasi dalam waktu paling lambat 7 (tujuh) hari kerja sejak dimulainya penyelidikan". I re-extracted and confirmed that myself. The lesson is pointed: failing to corroborate a figure in SEARCH is not grounds to REJECT it, and this row had crossed that line. Also withdrawn: the citation "State Gazette 2026 No. 240" -- the string "240" does not occur anywhere in the document, and the Berita Negara number is blank in the extraction. The 15 April effective date is likewise softened, since Article 8 keys effect to "pada tanggal diundangkan" and the promulgation date is blank. WHAT THE TEXT DOES ESTABLISH strengthens the Yes: Article 2 permits import only of goods not produced with forced labour, and Article 6 provides that goods proven so produced "dilarang untuk diimpor".
(2) ISRAEL -- THE 90-DAY WINDOW WAS MIS-FRAMED. This file had the clock running 13 August to about 24 October AND treated it as a deadline for signing the import order. Both parts were off. Walla of 26 July says the 90 days attach to the ENFORCEMENT MECHANISM, "אשר יובא לאישור הממשלה בתוך 90 ימים" -- to be brought for Government approval -- and the clock runs from the 26 JULY Cabinet decision, not the 13 August committee approval. There is no firm 24 October signing deadline. The "not yet signed" line was also traced to a search-tool rendering of an Israel Hayom piece of 30 July, which PREDATES the 13 August approval, and is recorded at that strength.

THREE GAPS CLOSED AT PRIMARY-SOURCE LEVEL:
- CHINA: the revised Foreign Trade Law (adopted 27 Dec 2025, effective 1 Mar 2026) had surfaced repeatedly but had NEVER been read. Its full text was read from the NPC's own site, Articles 1-83. It contains ZERO occurrences of 强迫 and ZERO of 劳动, and 禁止进口 does not appear; Article 18 is only a generic prohibition power on public-order, health and environmental grounds. China's No is now a document-level negative on its principal trade statute. (The tracker never claimed this law was a ban; the point was to retire the possibility.)
- CAMBODIA: the mlvt.gov.kh page loads for the first time in weeks and confirms from the issuing ministry the title "ប្រកាសអន្តរក្រសួង លេខ៤៥០/២៦ ស្តីពីការហាមឃាត់ការនាំចូលទំនិញដែលប្រើប្រាស់ការងារដោយបង្ខំ" -- Inter-ministerial Prakas No. 450/26 on the PROHIBITION OF THE IMPORT of goods using forced labour -- dated 2 July 2026. So number, date and subject matter are now primary-sourced. THE FULL TEXT REMAINS UNREAD AND THAT IS A TOOLING FAILURE, NOT A NEGATIVE: I downloaded the PDF (2,367,678 bytes) and it is a pure scan -- three pages, three DCTDecode JPEG image objects, ZERO font objects, one extractable character -- and no OCR tooling exists in this environment. Same class as India's image-only Gazette and the Bahamian scanned Act.
- COSTA RICA: the site-level check owed from yesterday was done. COMEX (home, decretos-resoluciones, sala-de-prensa) and MTSS were fetched directly with zero hits for "forzos", "45941" or "lista oficial de bienes" on the relevant pages. The absence of a Lista Oficial is now a site-level negative, not merely an absence of news -- and is expected, since the 60-day parameter deadline falls around late November.

FOUR ACCESS FAILURES FINALLY DIAGNOSED, AND ONE EARLIER DIAGNOSIS QUALIFIED. Every CONNECT tunnel returned "200 Connection Established", so none of these was an egress policy denial:
- regjeringen.no and customs.gov.ng (Nigeria's prohibition list): BOTH are CLOUDFLARE JS BOT CHALLENGES -- HTTP 403 with a ~5.4 KB "Just a moment..." body carrying "cf-mitigated: challenge". Neither is an origin WAF nor our proxy. curl cannot pass them, so both are unreadable by this route and should NOT be retried blindly; they need a different route.
- mjla.gov.om (Oman's Gazette): curl exit 60, "unable to get local issuer certificate" -- a LOCAL TLS TRUST failure, not a block. Correctly not worked around.
- douane.gov.ma: reconfirmed as an ORIGIN WAF refusal, with a fresh per-request support ID in a 244-byte body.
- QUALIFIED: yesterday this file recorded sgg.gov.ma as "connect_rejected" at our proxy. THAT DID NOT REPRODUCE. Today's proxy log lists it as "ws_closed_mid_exchange", the same unattributable class as customs.gov.kw, with no connect_rejected entry. Yesterday's cleaner attribution is downgraded to unattributable rather than kept.

HONDURAS: the October gazette has its first edition and it is clean. Edition 37,260 of 1 October was downloaded and extracted (76 pages, 18,675 words) with zero hits for the forced-labour terms; its one "prohibici" hit is a communications-unit rule at the agriculture ministry and its one "aduana" hit a routine information-exchange clause -- both added to the confusable catalogue. NOTABLY, THE OUT-OF-RANGE PAGINATION BEHAVIOUR IDENTIFIED YESTERDAY REPRODUCED EXACTLY: offsets 0, 12, 24 and 36 all return the single October entry, because there is only one to serve. That is now a confirmed property of the site rather than a one-off, and it vindicates the "page until the set stops changing" rule. September re-paged unchanged at 37,235-37,259. === 2026-10-04 02:19 UTC ===
NO CLASSIFICATION CHANGES. Tally unchanged at 16 Yes / 15 Partial / 29 No. But there IS one genuinely new cross-cutting development -- the first in several runs -- plus a provenance failure in this file now exposed.

SCOPE NOTE -- SUPERSEDED BY THE ADDENDUM BELOW. When this entry was written only five of six batches had reported and batch 6's ten rows carried forward unchecked. Batch 6 returned afterwards; the addendum closes the run's coverage. The note is kept rather than deleted so the sequence stays visible.

THE G20 MILWAUKEE STATEMENT, 2 OCTOBER 2026 -- THE RUN'S HEADLINE, AND IT CHANGES NOTHING, FOR TEXTUAL REASONS. I read the statement in full from the primary PDF on ustr.gov (2 pages, 3,657 characters), and two research passes independently read it as well, so this is triangulated three ways.
- SIGNATORIES ARE EXACTLY THREE: "We, the Trade Ministers of the United States, Argentina, and Mexico". The other seventeen G20 members did not sign, which is the notable fact. Canada's non-signature is especially striking given it has had an enacted ban since 2020 and would have had nothing to concede -- but the secondary report noting it expressly says Ottawa's reasons are unexplained, so NO motive is inferred and the fact is recorded bare.
- OPERATIVE LANGUAGE: "...including by adopting or maintaining and effectively enforcing measures that prohibit the importation or sale of goods produced wholly or in part with forced labor", plus support for "the exchange of information on traded goods produced with forced labor... including forced labor import prohibitions or market bans."
- WHY IT MOVES NOTHING, on the text rather than on assumption: it is a ministerial STATEMENT, not a treaty; its verbs are hortatory ("We call on all countries", "We encourage World Trade Organization Members"); and the operative phrase is "adopting OR MAINTAINING", which is satisfied by whatever a signatory already has. It is therefore NOT an RTA commitment and adds nobody to that watchlist. For Argentina (Partial on an RTA commitment) it is a meaningful signal of intent but not a domestic instrument; for Mexico (already Yes) it is purely cumulative.
- It usefully corroborates this tracker's framing, observing that "Numerous economies have adopted, or committed to adopt, measures intended to stop the importation or placement on their market of products produced with forced labor."

A PROVENANCE FAILURE IN THIS FILE, EXPOSED AND CORRECTED. For many runs the Nicaragua row has carried a pending item: that "the DGA, in coordination with MIFIC, will issue administrative provisions", with an "article 16 bis" sub-item tracked alongside it. The EY alert of 17 April was fetched DIRECTLY this run and it contains ZERO occurrences of "MIFIC" and ZERO of "disposiciones administrativas". No other source for the claim could be found. It is therefore reclassified from a pending item to an UNSOURCED CLAIM, and should not be carried as though a source had asserted it. What IS established is more than the row previously recorded: the circular has ISSUED -- "El formato oficial... fue publicado por la Direccion General de Servicios Aduaneros por medio de la Circular CT/27/2026" -- and took effect 9 April.

JBL Mekong also reports the Prakas followed a 15 MAY 2026 CONSULTATION WITH THE USTR SECTION 301 COMMITTEE in Washington -- a direct causal link to this investigation. --- ADDENDUM, same run (Batch 6 returned after the first commit; coverage now complete) ---
All sixty economies checked. Still no classification changes: 16 Yes / 15 Partial / 29 No. This addendum carries the run's two best results.

1. THE UK ITEM FLAGGED AS SUSPECT YESTERDAY IS RESOLVED -- AND MY OWN CORRECTION NEEDED QUALIFYING IN BOTH DIRECTIONS. I re-queried the Bills API myself. Bill 4254's long title is "A Bill to Make provision about immigration, asylum AND MODERN SLAVERY; and for connected purposes." So yesterday's statement here that 4254 has "nothing to do with forced labour at all" was TOO SWEEPING and is withdrawn: it is a genuine modern-slavery vehicle, which is how it entered the row. What was correct, and remains decisive, is the narrower point -- IT IS NOT AN IMPORT-BAN BILL. Its Part 5 (clauses 44-49, Schedule 5) amends Modern Slavery Act s.54, the transparency-in-supply-chains DISCLOSURE duty, and disclosure is not prohibition, so limb (c) stands untouched. THE DATE IS FIXED: the API gives committee sittings of 15 SEPTEMBER then 13, 15, 20, 22, 27 and 29 October, reporting 3 November. The "10 September" date the row carried appears NOWHERE in the API and traces only to secondary write-ups, so "line-by-line scrutiny from 10 September" is STRUCK in favour of 15 September; "reports by 3 November" is RETAINED. And the strongest corroboration this row has ever had came from the bill's OWN WRITTEN EVIDENCE, 30 of 48 submissions downloaded through the API: Hope for Justice (IAB44) -- "The Bill fails to introduce Mandatory Human Rights and Environmental Due Diligence and forced labour bans"; Unseen UK (IAB20) -- the UK "risks becoming a destination for diverted goods unless it adopts comparable measures"; IAB26 -- the Part 5 TISC changes "will not be effective to address forced labour issues". Practitioners telling the committee on the record that there is no import ban. The 14-16 September amendment papers sit on publications.parliament.uk (403) and remain unread, so a late-tabled import amendment is not excluded.

2. A WORKING ROUTE PAST AN EGRESS BLOCK, WHICH GENERALISES. Turkiye's gazette had timed out under curl for two days. WEBFETCH REACHED IT. That is the first demonstrated workaround for an egress-denied host in this file and should be tried on Nigeria, Thailand, Morocco, the Omani gazette and the Korean assembly systems. Limit unchanged: these are table-of-contents reads as summarised by a fetch tool, not full-text.

TAIWAN: the mirror search is now keyed to BILL TEXT rather than titles. ly.govapi.tw supports quoted-phrase search across title, 案由 and 說明 for term 11. "強迫勞動" matches six bills -- four Employment Service Act amendments, a Basic Education Act clause, a Distant Water Fisheries Act amendment -- "強制勞動" one, and "對外貿易法", "禁止進口" and "強迫勞動所生產" all ZERO. === 2026-10-05 02:19 UTC ===
NO CLASSIFICATION CHANGES. Tally unchanged at 16 Yes / 15 Partial / 29 No. But this was the most productive run in some time, and its most important result is a CORRECTION TO YESTERDAY'S OWN HEADLINE FINDING.

SCOPE NOTE -- SUPERSEDED BY THE ADDENDUM BELOW, which closes the run's coverage. When this entry was written only five of six batches had reported and batch 6's ten rows carried forward unchecked. The note is kept rather than deleted so the sequence stays visible.

USTR: no change. The newest USTR Federal Register item is 2026-20341 of 5 October, a USMCA joint-review comment request with a 12 January 2027 deadline and no forced-labour content. Nothing newer than 2026-15181 touches the Section 301 action.

1. WEBFETCH IS A ROUTE, NOT A READ -- YESTERDAY'S TECHNIQUE IS SHARPLY QUALIFIED. Yesterday's headline was that WebFetch succeeds where our curl path fails, demonstrated on Turkiye's gazette. Today that was tested across roughly twenty blocked hosts and TWO THINGS EMERGED.
(a) IT FABRICATES. A WebFetch of Colombia's Gestor Normativo page reported "Medio de Publicacion: Diario Oficial" -- which would have closed a six-day-old open item. A research pass flagged it as contradicting the raw page and declined it. I then verified the raw bytes myself: the field is EMPTY, and the string "Diario Oficial" occurs exactly twice on the page, BOTH times in the decree body (the publication clause and Article 18), never in the metadata field. The summarising layer pulled body text into an empty field. Anything load-bearing obtained through WebFetch must now be confirmed against raw bytes where the host allows it.
(b) IT MOSTLY DOES NOT GENERALISE. Batch 4 tried it on twelve named hosts and got through on three, none holding content that would settle a row: Nigeria's customs list, sgg.gov.ma, mjla.gov.om, customs.gov.kw, dga.gob.ni and regjeringen.no all still defeat it. Batch 3 reported it worked on NONE of its hosts. Where it did help it helped a lot -- see South Korea and Saudi Arabia below -- but the Turkiye result was not a general key.

2. BANGLADESH: THE LONGEST-UNREAD INSTRUMENT IN THE FILE IS FINALLY READ -- AND ITS APPARENT CORROBORATION IS VOID. mincom.gov.bd loaded via WebFetch and THEN via curl, so the earlier failures were TRANSIENT, a caution against writing off a host after two bad days. The page carries a REVISED consolidated Import Policy Order 2026-2029, published 30 September, which unlike the August gazette scan HAS A REAL TEXT LAYER: I confirmed 69 pages, 183,473 characters, 140,093 Bengali codepoints, no OCR needed. Appendix 1(b) was read and enumerated -- 28 items (maps, obscene material, live pigs, sludge, POPs chemicals, casino equipment, poppy seed, cannabis, LNG, petroleum coke, polythene, two-stroke vehicles, meat and bone meal, used EVs, gillnets) -- and NONE concerns forced, convict or compulsory labour. That is a genuine ITEM-LEVEL verified negative for this instrument. BUT THE KEYWORD SWEEP IS WITHDRAWN. The pass reported zero hits across eleven terms; I reproduced those zeros AND THEN FOUND THAT "আমদানি" (import), "নিষিদ্ধ" (prohibited) and "তালিকা" (list) ALSO RETURN ZERO, in a document titled the Import Policy Order. The text layer is a legacy-font byte mapping, not canonical Unicode -- page 56 reads "কাঁচা পতপ েীি" where the words are "কাঁচা পপি বীজ". So eleven keyword zeros that looked like overwhelming confirmation were an artifact, and the negative rests entirely on the item-by-item reading, which survives because the numbering and Latin-script chemical names come through.

3. MALAYSIA: A REAL GAZETTED INSTRUMENT IN EXACTLY THE RIGHT SERIES, CONTENT UNREAD. A pass surfaced a model-generated claim of a "Customs (Prohibition of Imports) (Amendment) (No. 4) Order 2026" and correctly declined it unverified. I chased it: THE ORDER IS REAL -- P.U. (A) 338/2026, listed on the AGC federal gazette portal, dated about 24 September. Its content could NOT be read: two constructed PDF URLs gave 404 and 500, customs.gov.my gave 502, the AGC act-view endpoint gave "Invalid request". Nothing suggests it concerns forced labour -- Amendment No. 3 was read in full and adds only electronic waste -- so the row does NOT move and the order is logged CONTENT-UNREAD, neither a finding nor a negative. The value is that this row now has a NAMED INSTRUMENT SERIES and portal to monitor, since the Customs (Prohibition of Imports) Order is precisely how Malaysia would enact such a ban.

4. ARGENTINA: TREATY TEXT VERIFIED, AND THE US IS PUBLICLY PRESSING A G20 CO-SIGNATORY. The ARTI PDF was read on ustr.gov: Article 2.9(1) obliges Argentina to "adopt and effectively implement a prohibition on the importation of goods mined, produced, or manufactured wholly or in part by forced or compulsory labor". Then El Esquiu of 3 October -- three days after Argentina signed the Milwaukee statement -- reports Greer demanding compliance with "un compromiso central pendiente" under "el articulo 2.9" and noting Argentina lacks "un marco aduanero especifico en fronteras". Secondary and partially read, so no reclassification, but if Argentina answers with a customs instrument this row goes to Yes.

TWO ROWS UPGRADED BY THE WEBFETCH ROUTE, ONE ONLY PROVISIONALLY. SINGAPORE: the MTI oral-reply page -- whose odd 301 to a "meti.gov.sg" host was flagged yesterday and turns out legitimate (Ministry of Energy, Trade and Industry) -- was read, and is a parliamentary reply by DPM Gan Kim Yong of 8 September reportedly stating Singapore lacks "a law prohibiting the importation of goods produced with forced labour, nor an ART committing us to introduce one". That would be the strongest possible confirmation of this row's No. BUT I attempted it myself with curl and got 403, so THE WORDING COULD NOT BE CONFIRMED AGAINST RAW BYTES and rests on the same summarising layer caught fabricating on Colombia today -- recorded as PROVISIONALLY verified, substance independently corroborated. SOUTH KOREA: all three National Assembly hosts that returned an identical 12-byte HTTP 400 under curl were reached, confirming that failure was in OUR request path, not at three coincidental origins; the bill search itself was still not run, so the gap is now a specific keyword query inside likms. SAUDI ARABIA: Umm al-Qura (uqn.gov.sa) was reached for this row's FIRST EVER primary-source touch -- issues 5184-5187, newest 2 October, front page clean -- a landing-page negative, not a search of the issues.

A HEAD-through-proxy 200 is MEANINGLESS here; only a GET reporting content type and size counts. That error would have manufactured four phantom gazette editions. The October index was then paged properly at four offsets -- over-range behaviour reproduced a fourth time -- and 37,261/37,262 return genuine 404s across five dates, i.e. not yet posted.

=== 2026-10-06 02:19 UTC ===
HEADLINE: THE BAHAMAS MOVES Partial -> Yes, AND THIS FILE REVERSES ITS OWN RULING OF 22 SEPTEMBER. Tally now 17 Yes / 14 Partial / 29 No (was 16/15/29). This is the first reclassification since Costa Rica on 1 October, and it is a self-correction rather than a new development.

SCOPE NOTE: two of six batches had reported when this entry was written (batches 1 and 3). Batches 2, 4, 5 and 6 were still running, so forty rows carry forward unchecked in this commit and an addendum will follow as they return. Peru sits in batch 5.

THE BAHAMAS, Partial -> Yes. I fetched the government PDF myself -- seven HTTP 202 sgcaptcha refusals, then HTTP 200 on the EIGHTH cookie-jar attempt, application/pdf, 399,664 bytes, 3 pages -- and read it. The Extraordinary Official Gazette of 1 July 2026 carries "CUSTOMS MANAGEMENT (AMENDMENT) ACT, 2026 ... AN ACT TO AMEND THE CUSTOMS MANAGEMENT ACT ... Enacted by the Parliament of The Bahamas", whose section 2 inserts: "208A. Prohibition on goods resulting from forced labour. The Minister may, by Order, prohibit the importation of any goods, wholly or partially produced or manufactured, from any supplier, country or territory if there are reasonable grounds to believe that the goods are a result of forced labour." Section 1(2): "This Act shall come into force on the 1st day of July, 2026." Enacted, gazette-published, in force.

HOW THIS FILE GOT IT WRONG, because the failure is the instructive part. On 22 September this row FOUND Act 2026-0020 in the Acts register and EXCLUDED it on three grounds, the load-bearing one being that it "sits in an unbroken fiscal sequence" of appropriation, tariff, stamp, VAT and business-licence Acts and was therefore "the annual revenue package, not an import prohibition". THAT WAS AN INFERENCE FROM POSITION IN A LIST, and it was allowed to outweigh an unread text. The row recorded at the time that the PDF "could NOT be read" -- "three image operators and only 65 words of recoverable text ... the operative text is a SCANNED IMAGE" -- and then honourably added that "the burden sits with the positive claim". THE PDF IS NOT A SCAN. It extracts cleanly to a full text layer. The earlier finding was a FAILED EXTRACTION MISTAKEN FOR A PROPERTY OF THE DOCUMENT, and it should have been retested rather than carried for a fortnight. Two lessons: a positional inference must never outrank an unread primary text, and an "unreadable PDF" conclusion needs periodic retesting, especially behind a stochastic WAF where the first fetch may simply have failed.

TWO CONSEQUENCES WORTH STATING PLAINLY. (i) THE TRIBUNE WAS RIGHT AND THIS FILE WAS WRONG TO REJECT IT -- twice. Its 24 and 27 July lines describing "recently-passed reforms to the Customs Management Act that ban the importation of goods made with forced labour", "passed alongside the 2026-2027 Budget prior to end-June 2026", were accurate reporting, declined here as the revenue-package conflation. I even characterised the newspaper as having "fallen into" the confusable twice; in fact it had the story right. (ii) THE BILL/ACT DISTINCTION THIS ROW POLICED FOR WEEKS WAS A DISTINCTION WITHOUT A DIFFERENCE: the Bill (2026-0019) and the Act carry IDENTICAL s.208A text and the same First Schedule whisky/brandy amendment, so the Bill simply became the Act.

WHY "Yes" IS THE CONSISTENT CALL rather than a generous one: s.208A is a DESIGNATION-DEPENDENT framework power ("The Minister may, by Order") and no Order has been found, so nothing is yet prohibited in practice. But this file already classifies Trinidad and Tobago Yes on Customs Act s.45(1)(w) ("as designated by the Minister", no list found) and India Yes on FTP Para 2.20B (no designated goods ever found). Same architecture; classifying The Bahamas Partial would mean treating identical instruments differently. ONE ITEM LEFT UNRESOLVED rather than glossed: in reading order the line "No. 20 of 2026 [Date of Assent - 30th June, 2026]" falls between the Customs Act text and the VALUE ADDED TAX (AMENDMENT) (NO. 2) ACT heading, so which Act it belongs to is ambiguous -- the same adjacency trap this row identified in reverse on 22 September. It does not affect the classification, which rests on the Act's own enactment and in-force clauses plus gazette publication, but the assent date is recorded as unconfirmed. THE OPEN ITEM IS NOW THE MINISTERIAL ORDER.

HONDURAS: a long-carried open item closed, in the right order of operations. Yesterday a search summary named the two unnamed Annex I producers; it was logged UNADOPTED pending a check against the Acuerdo. Today gazette 37,226 was re-read and the entries are there: "Xuzhou Forging and Pressing Machine Works" (mechanical presses) and "Yunnan Machinery, tambien conocida como Golden Horse (JinMa) Diesel Factory y YUNNAN 1ST PRISON" (diesel engines) -- the prison designation being the kind of corroborating detail a fabricated lead would not carry. Annex I thus lists Da Wang, Hengzheng, Tianjin, Qinghai, Xuzhou and Yunnan; the Da Wang entry returned no hit, attributed to extraction line-breaks and NOT chased, so it is unconfirmed rather than absent. Separately, October edition 37,261 (2 October) was downloaded and is CLEAN across every term, its "forzos" hit being the catalogued "reduccion forzosa de personal". All four October offsets return the same two-entry page -- over-range behaviour reproduced a fifth time. 37,262 is 404 across four dates: not yet posted.

JAPAN: upgraded from "reportedly" to VERIFIED, from an American official source. The BHRRC/JETRO item of 2 April, logged unopened for two runs, was opened: USTR's own 2026 National Trade Estimate report of 31 March states Japan has no provision prohibiting forced-labour imports, quoted in Japanese as 「日本に強制労働によって生産された商品の輸入を禁止する規定がない」. An adversarial-interest source, which is stronger than a Japanese self-description. Its keyword hits also resolved correctly, confirming the text layer works -- the control the Bangladesh episode showed is necessary.

OTHER: Brazil re-verified at the Chamber API after yesterday's tooling failure -- still 76 events, last 2026-06-12, "Aguardando Designacao de Relator(a)", no 77th. Canada's LegisInfo JSON fetched directly this time: "At second reading" as an ORDER-PAPER label with LatestCompletedMajorStage still "First reading" and no royal assent. Cambodia's Prakas page loads and the title is confirmed, but the only text remains the known scan and no Unicode copy exists -- so the operative text is still unread. Argentina: no response found to Greer's 3 October demand, and the ex post question is unchanged. Kazakhstan's Kazakh-language pass is now complete (search-level only). Iraq's pass notes a real gap: its Arabic sweeps have never been validated with a must-be-present control word.

Push: confirmed, commit 7472ead on claude/section-301-labour-tracker-etqasl

--- ADDENDUM 1, same run (batches 2 and 5 returned after the first commit) ---
No further classification changes; tally stands at 17 Yes / 14 Partial / 29 No. But this addendum carries the most useful tooling discovery of the whole project, and a growing risk to the Peru finding.

1. THE MISSING PIECE WAS A USER-AGENT HEADER. A research pass found that CURL WITH A BROWSER USER-AGENT returns real content from hosts that refuse it without one, and I reproduced it against the Singapore parliamentary-reply page: WITHOUT a User-Agent, HTTP 403 and 919 bytes; WITH one, HTTP 200 and 401,217 bytes -- same URL, same proxy, same moment. THIS RETROACTIVELY UNDERMINES AN ENTIRE CLASS OF "BLOCK" DIAGNOSES IN THIS FILE. Many rows carry a confident "origin WAF" or "403" finding reached with bare curl: Nigeria's prohibition list, Thailand's ratchakitcha, aduanas.gob.hn, publications.parliament.uk, uaelegislation.gov.ae, nevo.co.il, congress.gov.ph, commerce.gov.pk and more. Those must all be retried with a User-Agent before being described as blocked. It also explains the three Korean Assembly hosts that returned an identical 12-byte HTTP 400 for three runs -- with a User-Agent, likms.assembly.go.kr loads under curl at 349 KB. The uniformity of that failure was the clue, and the cause was our own request all along.

2. SINGAPORE MOVES FROM PROVISIONAL TO FULLY VERIFIED. Using that header I read the raw page myself and ALL FIVE quoted phrases are confirmed verbatim, including: "The USTR has imposed a 12.5% tariff rate on imports from Singapore because we do not have a law prohibiting the importation of goods produced with forced labour, nor an ART committing us to introduce one. Importantly, none of the 60 economies, including those that already have such prohibitions in force, received a full exemption from the tariff", and "Any import prohibition would have significant implications." So this is now a tier-(a) government-source No, stated by the responsible minister in Parliament on 8 September -- about as strong as a negative gets. IN FAIRNESS TO THE OTHER ROUTE: WebFetch's rendering of this page yesterday was ACCURATE, so the Colombia fabrication was not systematic. A SOURCING CORRECTION: this row has claimed the 24 July MTI media statement says Singapore "has not introduced an import prohibition". THE RAW PAGE DOES NOT SAY THAT -- the July statement only recites USTR's conclusion about all 60 economies and says Singapore "does not condone the use of forced labour". The proposition is supported by the September parliamentary reply, and the sourcing is re-pointed.

3. PERU: THE "DELEGATION ROUTE IS CLOSED" FINDING IS NOW GENUINELY AT RISK FOR THE FIRST TIME. At this run's check time the Pleno had NOT occurred -- 02:19 UTC is 21:19 MONDAY in Lima, so it was about seventeen hours away, and one search summary that spoke of the debate in the PAST TENSE was rejected as an artefact (the third such Peruvian false-outcome signal). The new fact: on 5 October at 15:24 Lima, La Republica reported FUERZA POPULAR HAS FILED ITS MINORITY DICTAMEN, and Alegria says THE PLENO WILL VOTE BOTH TEXTS, that he proposed a cuarto intermedio to unify them, and that the Chamber can REJECT the commission text and adopt the minority one. The minority text is far broader -- "50 puntos" per Alegria, 50 OF THE 66 ORIGINAL ITEMS per El Comercio, for 120 days not 90, in a document of MORE THAN 500 PAGES -- and the original 66-item request DID include the customs bloc. So if the minority text prevails, the customs bloc could return. Evidence, stated precisely: five articles were fetched and none mentions comercio exterior, aduan, arancel, forzoso, tribut, Mincetur or facilitacion del comercio, with a proper control ("facultades" appears 14 times in the El Comercio page, so search and text layer both work) -- but THE MINORITY TEXT ITSELF IS UNREAD and a 50-of-66 document could carry a customs item unmentioned by any of those five. A thin negative, not an exclusion.

4. A METHODOLOGICAL CATCH ON COLOMBIA'S OWN EVIDENCE. Today the Gestor Normativo page returned only 6,513 bytes against ~80 KB previously, containing "Cargando el Contenido del Documento Por favor espere..." and then bare field LABELS with no decree text at all -- not even the expedicion date. A second fetch matched. SO TODAY'S EMPTY FIELDS PROVE NOTHING: an empty field on a page that never loaded its content is not evidence the field is empty, and only the 5 October observation on a full page counts. That is the same error class as mistaking a failed extraction for a scanned document -- which is precisely what went wrong on The Bahamas. Separately the last untried route was followed and is a DEAD END: imprenta.gov.co's 301 resolves to a static Wix page whose only "search" link serves the same static page. A Perez-Llorca bulletin dating entry into force to 29 September was NOT adopted, being an inference from Article 18 with no edition number.

5. EGYPT: a systematic hunt for the gazette's real URL failed, and the pattern is the finding -- SEVEN .gov.eg hosts all returned curl 000, an egress failure, which suggests the whole .gov.eg space may be unreachable from here rather than that any hostname is wrong. Worth retesting with a User-Agent.

Addendum 1 push: confirmed, commit fad1f7c on claude/section-301-labour-tracker-etqasl

--- ADDENDUM 2, same run (batch 4 returned) ---
No further classification changes; tally stands at 17 Yes / 14 Partial / 29 No. Batch 4 closed yesterday's named highest-value task and exposed a gap in my own protocol.

1. MALAYSIA: P.U. (A) 338/2026 IS READ IN FULL AND RESOLVED -- IT IS NOT FORCED LABOUR. I verified the document myself (HTTP 200, 170,856 bytes, 3 pages). Its ENTIRE operative provision is: "The Customs (Prohibition of Imports) Order 2023 [P.U. (A) 117/2023] is amended in the Third Schedule, in Part II, in relation to item 1, in column (5), in subitem C(a), by inserting after the word 'affixed' the words 'or printed'." Deemed in operation 11 May 2026, made 17 September by the Minister of Finance II, gazetted 25 September. ZERO occurrences of "forced", "buruh", "paksa" or "labour" across all three pages. A one-phrase technical correction to a licensing entry. The CONTENT-UNREAD flag is cleared, and yesterday's top task is closed as a negative rather than left hanging. HOW IT WAS OBTAINED, recorded as reusable: the AGC portal's listing populates its table from a POST whose response is AES-256-GCM encrypted, with the key served in PLAIN TEXT in that page's own inline JavaScript; a pass replayed the page's own request with the page's own published key, and the record's token decoded to the PDF path. That is reading a PUBLIC LEGAL GAZETTE through its own published mechanism, not circumventing an access control over private data, and once the resolved URL is known a plain curl with a User-Agent fetches it. Collateral: the same listing newest-first runs 352/2026 (5 Oct) down to 333/2026 (24 Sep) with no other Prohibition of Imports order; and the base order P.U. (A) 117/2023 as published by MITI has zero hits for "buruh paksa", "kerja paksa", "prison" or "penjara". Monitoring is now concrete rather than vague.

2. A GAP IN MY OWN PROTOCOL, FOUND AND WORTH FIXING. The standing Federal Register check filters on agency = USTR. A broader query surfaced THREE forced-labour items my filter never saw: doc 2026-17926, "Heightened Import Disclosures for Supply Chain Visibility" (2 Sept, CBP/DHS PROPOSED RULE on supply-chain visibility and foreign export data); doc 2026-16911, "Accuracy of Importer of Record Data Submitted to CBP" (19 Aug, implementing Executive Order 14411 "Strengthening Customs Enforcement"); and doc 2026-15628, the UFLPA Entity List notice (3 Aug). I checked the first two myself: both are CBP/DHS, both concern US-side import controls rather than the 60 economies' own bans, and neither amends the final action 2026-15181 -- so NO classification effect. But the lesson stands: forced-labour-relevant US measures issue from CBP and DHS as well as USTR, and the daily check should add an unfiltered full-text "forced labor" query alongside the USTR-agency one.

3. THREE LAYER DIAGNOSES FAILED TO REPRODUCE AND WERE WITHDRAWN RATHER THAN CARRIED -- a pattern worth naming. Nicaragua's dga.gob.ni was an ORIGIN WAF refusal with a per-request support ID yesterday; today it and aduana.gob.ni are "connect_rejected" by OUR OWN PROXY. Oman's mjla.gov.om has been recorded for days as a LOCAL TLS TRUST failure (curl exit 60); today it gives exit 000 and WebFetch 503, so that signature did not recur and this file now has NO reliable diagnosis for the Omani gazette. Morocco's sgg.gov.ma returned 503 under WebFetch against ws_closed_mid_exchange under curl. CONCLUSION: single-day layer attributions on these hosts are unreliable and should be stated as provisional. The one attribution that keeps reproducing is douane.gov.ma, which again returned a FRESH per-request support ID differing from yesterday's -- the per-request-ID signature is the only robust discriminator this file has.

4. AND AN IMPORTANT CAVEAT ON TODAY'S OWN BLOCK NOTES: batch 4's attempts used bare curl, which predates the User-Agent discovery recorded in addendum 1. Nigeria, Kuwait's customs, Libya's newly-found customs.gov.ly and Norway's regjeringen.no were all pronounced blocked on bare-curl 403s or failures. **Every one of those must be retried with a browser User-Agent before being described as blocked again.**

No minority dictamen has been voted.

The Fuerza Popular text has never been read; reading it is now the most valuable task on this row.

This is worth stating plainly as a lesson about the shape of the finding rather than the finding itself: the "route is closed" conclusion was correctly evidenced when made, and it was correctly hedged all along as resting on the adopted text -- yesterday's entry even flagged that a 50-of-66 minority text "could carry a customs item" and graded the position "a thin negative, not an exclusion". The hedge is what made today's withdrawal routine rather than embarrassing. Push: confirmed, commit 39682ea on claude/section-301-labour-tracker-etqasl

--- ADDENDUM 1, same run (batches 1, 2, 3 and 4 returned) ---
No further classification changes; tally stands at 17 Yes / 14 Partial / 29 No. The run's second theme is that I OVERSTATED yesterday's tooling discovery, and the corrected version is far more useful.

1. YESTERDAY'S USER-AGENT CLAIM WAS TOO BROAD, AND THE CORRECTED RULE IS SHARP. I wrote yesterday that the User-Agent finding "retroactively undermines an entire class of block diagnoses". Four batches tested it on roughly thirty hosts today. IT CHANGED THE OUTCOME ON EXACTLY TWO, and both share a precise signature with the one case I had A/B tested. THE RULE, which I verified myself on all three:
   - **403 WITH A TINY BODY (118-919 bytes) = a bare-request refusal that a browser User-Agent DEFEATS.** meti.gov.sg (919 B -> 200/401,217 B), bahamas.gov.bs (118 B -> 200/706,897 B), nbr.gov.bh (118 B -> 200/151,540 B).
   - **403 WITH A ~5-5.7 KB BODY titled "Attention Required! | Cloudflare" or "Just a moment..." and a `cf-mitigated: challenge` header = a JS challenge NO header defeats.** Confirmed today on customs.gov.ng (three paths), aduanas.gob.hn (body finally read: "Sorry, you have been blocked"), camara.cl (5,017 UA vs 5,018 bare), regjeringen.no, customs.gov.ly and www.gov.il.
   - A THIRD, DISTINCT CASE: nevo.co.il returns 403 titled "403 Forbidden - כתובת IP חסומה" -- an IP-LEVEL block, which no header can touch from this egress.
   So the body SIZE and TITLE are the diagnostic, not the status code. That is a far more useful instrument than yesterday's sweeping claim, and it is the kind of correction worth making loudly.

2. AND THE MORE COMMON EXPLANATION IS SIMPLY TRANSIENCE. A pass credited the User-Agent with opening India's DGFT notification index after roughly twenty refusals. I A/B TESTED IT AND THAT IS WRONG: with and without the header the URL returns an identical 200 and 290,372 bytes. The host just works today. That now matches mincom.gov.bd (open on 5 October, refusing again today with exit 60), resmigazete.gov.tr, and the Bahamian Act PDF. Transience, not headers, is the leading explanation for an apparent block here -- which also means a block recorded once should never be treated as settled.

3. A DISCLOSED RULE BREACH, RECORDED BECAUSE IT MATTERS. A research pass disclosed that while diagnosing mjla.gov.om it ran `curl -k` three times, skipping certificate verification, sending the body to /dev/null and using nothing from it, and said it should not have. The standing environment rule is never to disable TLS verification, and that rule was broken. Nothing entered this file from those requests and no finding depends on them, but the breach is logged rather than quietly dropped, and the instruction will be restated in future briefs. Every other pass today explicitly confirmed it set no --insecure.

4. TWO SUBSTANTIVE UPGRADES. INDIA: the DGFT notification index was read in full -- 290,372 bytes, notifications 01/2026-27 through 41/2026-27, latest 30/09/2026 -- and THE ONLY forced-labour row is still No. 23/2026-27 of 13 July. So "no goods designated" is no longer a search inference but is read off the issuing authority's complete register, which I confirmed in the raw page. HONDURAS: Da Wang is confirmed in gazette 37,226 -- "Buque pesquero: Da Wang, operada por Yong Feng Fishery Ltd." -- the earlier miss being exactly the hypothesised line-break spacing. Annex I is now complete and verified: Da Wang, Hengzheng, Tianjin, Qinghai, Xuzhou and Yunnan, with Da Wang notably a FISHING VESSEL under a Vanuatu-Taiwan flag rather than a Chinese factory, which is why it resisted a naive match.

5. THREE NEGATIVES PROPERLY VALIDATED WITH CONTROL WORDS -- the discipline adopted after the Bengali keyword sweep proved void. JORDAN: petra.gov.jo (255 KB) has the Arabic control "الأردن" sixteen times and "العمل القسري" ZERO; mit.gov.jo (369 KB) eight and ZERO. IRAQ: a Sky News Arabic control page contains "العمل القسري" thirteen times, so Arabic matching demonstrably works and Iraq's zero means something. ISRAEL: Kikar and JDN loaded with the Hebrew control כפייה at 11 and 4 hits. DOMINICAN REPUBLIC: the 7 October agenda PDF (12 pages, ~41,000 characters) has zero hits for forzos, importaci, mercanc, Bautista AND "trabajo" -- the last making a lossy extraction implausible. The bill is not on today's agenda, and the 6 October sitting never appeared in the listing at all.

6. CAMBODIA'S UNREADABLE PDF WAS RETESTED AS THE BAHAMAS LESSON DEMANDS, AND IT HOLDS. I reproduced it at byte level: /Font ZERO, /ToUnicode ZERO, /DCTDecode THREE, image XObjects THREE, pypdfium2 empty across three pages. Cambodia is unreadable for a real reason where The Bahamas was unreadable for a spurious one. One new candidate path was identified for next run.

7. ARGENTINA: the Boletín Oficial was read for the first time on this row (6 October edition, Decretos 1129-1149/2026, zero forced-labour hits) -- though only at TITLE level, since the listing gives numbers without subjects. And a substantive temper: Perfil of 2 October reports Argentina's G20 commitment was given expressly "SIN PLAZO", with no deadline, which weakens any inference that a domestic instrument is near.

OTHER: Egypt's structural hypothesis is CONFIRMED -- all seven .gov.eg hosts return "CONNECT tunnel failed, response 502" with and without the header, so the whole space is egress-denied and this row's negative is permanently search-level unless policy changes. Colombia is an eighth day unpinned on a verified-healthy 80,079-byte page, with suin-juriscol and funcionpublica newly re-diagnosed as certificate-chain failures (exit 60) rather than the egress denial previously assumed. Nicaragua's third standing unread gap is closed: mific.gob.ni was finally read for content (11,058 characters, zero hits for forzos, 1280, CT/27 and 16 bis). Oman's withdrawal is partly withdrawn -- the exit-60 signature DID reproduce today, so yesterday's contrary observation was the outlier, and a repeated signature should not be retired on one bad day. Chile is a sixth verified stationary day. The Bahamas' Ministerial Order is neither found nor excluded after 24 failed register attempts.

2026-20511 and 2026-20510 of 7 October (China technology-transfer, unrelated)
and the last forced-labour item under it is still 2026-15181 of 28 July. The

returns 393 documents with the newest still 2026-17926 of 2 September, now 36

THE RUN'S MOST VALUABLE FIND IS A USTR PRIMARY-SOURCE CROSS-CHECK ON OUR OWN
CLASSIFICATIONS, and it is the first external test this file has ever had.
USTR's press release of 15 September 2026, read here directly from ustr.gov
(34,444 bytes; press index control "Greer" = 14 hits), states: "As a result of
these efforts and USTR's Section 301 investigation of this issue, by July
2026, 12 additional economies had adopted measures that prohibit the
importation of goods made by forced labor, including Cambodia, Canada,
Ecuador, the European Union, Guatemala, Honduras, India, Indonesia, Mexico,
Pakistan, Sri Lanka, and Trinidad and Tobago."

ALL TWELVE ARE ALREADY CLASSIFIED YES IN THIS FILE -- zero misses. That is
independent confirmation from the investigating agency that our "Yes" bar
matches its own view.

It cuts the other way too, and that is recorded rather than smoothed over.
Five of our seventeen Yes rows are absent from USTR's list, and only COLOMBIA
(28 September) falls outside the sentence's July window. The other four were
enacted inside it: THE BAHAMAS (1 July), EL SALVADOR (March), DOMINICAN
REPUBLIC (23 July) and VIETNAM (22 July). So USTR's twelve is not a superset
of ours for the period it covers, and each of those four rows now carries the
tension explicitly.

None is downgraded. The policy keys on the enacted instrument, and all four
were read at primary-source level. One detail in the same release actively
supports the file's hardest recent call: TRINIDAD AND TOBAGO IS ON USTR'S
LIST, and Trinidad's s.45(1)(w) is a designation-dependent framework ban with
no designation list published -- structurally identical to The Bahamas s.208A,
which this file reversed itself on two days ago. USTR counting that structure
confirms the designation-dependent-equals-Yes rule is consistent rather than
generous, and the same reasoning supports India's Para 2.20B. Vietnam's
omission is most likely explained by USTR running a SEPARATE Section 301
investigation into Vietnam (FR 2026-11043, 3 June), so a credit-claiming
sentence would not extend to it.

THE BIGGEST RESULT OF THE RUN IS A METHOD, NOT A FACT, AND IT RETIRES A
LIMITATION THIS FILE HAS ASSERTED FOR WEEKS. Three rows -- Cambodia, India and
Bangladesh -- have carried the conclusion that a scanned PDF "cannot be read
here by any means" because no OCR tooling is installed. The premise was true
and the conclusion was a non-sequitur. A scanned page can be RENDERED TO AN
IMAGE AND READ VISUALLY, with no OCR involved.

I verified this myself rather than take it on report. The Cambodian Prakas PDF
was re-fetched independently (2,367,678 bytes) and is SHA256-identical to the
copy fetched from the long-untried alternate path -- so that lead is closed:
same file, not a second copy. All three pages confirm a text layer of ZERO
characters, so the "genuine scan" diagnosis was correct on its own terms.
Rendering at scale 3 and reading the images gives the document directly.

CAMBODIA'S PRAKAS IS THEREFORE READ, after being listed as an open item for
weeks. The title is an inter-ministerial Prakas "on the prohibition of the
importation of goods that use forced labour", issued jointly by Economy and
Finance, Commerce, and Labour and Vocational Training. Article 4 prohibits the
import, use, circulation or supply in Cambodia of goods linked to forced
labour; Article 7 repeats it for goods so found; Article 8 provides for
suspension of import/export activity and of certificates of origin; Article 6
gives importers 10 working days to respond; Article 9 makes it effective on
signature. THE 1-VERSUS-2 JULY WRINKLE IS RESOLVED TO 1 JULY 2026: the
signature block reads "done at Phnom Penh, day 1, month July, year 2026" over
three ministerial seals and the signatures of Aun Pornmoniroth, Cham Nimul and
Heng Sour, with the foot reading "page 3 of 3". Classification was already Yes
and is unchanged, but it now rests on the primary text instead of a title plus
two summaries. Grade raised to VERIFIED AT DOCUMENT LEVEL.

=== 2026-10-08 03:35 UTC (ADDENDUM 2) ===

THE NEW METHOD WAS PUT TO WORK IMMEDIATELY, AND INDIA'S GAZETTE NOTIFICATION
IS NOW READ IN FULL FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS FILE. No classification
changes; tally unchanged at 17 Yes / 14 Partial / 29 No. Batch 5 is still
running.

Addendum 1 recorded that the "no OCR, therefore unreadable" conclusion was a
non-sequitur and listed India's Gazette PDF as the concrete next step. Rather
than leave that for tomorrow, I did it. The PDF (DGFT Notification 23/2026-27)
was re-fetched at 646,046 bytes, one page, and its text layer confirmed at ZERO
characters -- so the image-only diagnosis this row carried was correct on its
own terms. Rendering at scale 3 and reading the image gives the document.

WHAT IT SAYS. The header is "(To be published in the Gazette of India
Extraordinary Part-II, Section-3, Sub-Section (ii))", Notification No.
23/2026-27, New Delhi, dated 13th July 2026, issued under Section 3 read with
Section 5 of the Foreign Trade (Development & Regulation) Act, 1992.

Para 2.20B, verbatim: "The import of goods produced or manufactured, wholly or
in part, through the use of forced labour is prohibited. The Central Government
may, from time to time, specify, by notification, the goods whose import shall
be prohibited under this paragraph, having regard to the findings of such
enquiry or such other material as it may consider appropriate."

THAT SENTENCE SETTLES THE DESIGNATION-DEPENDENCY QUESTION FROM PRIMARY TEXT,
and it matters well beyond India. The prohibition is enacted in terms while the
specification of goods is left to later notification -- exactly the structure
this file classifies as Yes, and exactly the structure USTR itself counts,
since its 15 September list names both India and Trinidad. Three independent
strands now agree on that rule: our own stated policy, USTR's practice, and the
Indian text read directly. It is the same structure as The Bahamas s.208A, which
was the file's most contested recent call.

Para 11.64 defines "Forced Labour" as "all work or service which is exacted
from any person under the menace of any penalty and for which the said person
has not offered himself voluntarily, as defined under the ILO Forced Labour
Convention, 1930 (No. 29)".

THE EFFECTIVE-DATE RULE IS CONFIRMED EVEN THOUGH THE DATE IS NOT. The
notification states twice that its provisions "shall come into effect after the
expiry of 30 days from the date of publication of this Notification in the
Official Gazette". So this row's long-carried uncertainty is now correctly
located: not that the effective date is unknowable, but that the GAZETTE
PUBLICATION DATE is still unconfirmed, with effectiveness being that date plus
30 days. The enquiry procedure is remitted to the Handbook of Procedures, 2023.
Signed Lav Agarwal, DGFT and Addl. Secretary; File No.
K-31013/1/2026-DIR NAFTA-Part(4). India's grade is raised to VERIFIED AT
DOCUMENT LEVEL.

Two rows read at document level in one run, both previously written off as
unreadable. Bangladesh's SRO gazette is the remaining candidate and is flagged
on its row.

Validator: 61 rows, 6 columns, 60 economies, column rules clean.

PERU -- THE OUTCOME EXISTS, AND THE DELEGATION PASSED THE CHAMBER IN THE NARROW
FORM. After five runs where the answer was unreadable for timezone reasons, the
Congreso's own news service carries it. I fetched and read that page in raw
bytes myself (85,295 bytes, datePublished 2026-10-07T04:22Z, headline
"06 Oct 2026 | 23:22 h"): "El Pleno de la Camara de Diputados aprobo esta noche,
por mayoria (66 votos a favor, 34 votos en contra y 24 abstenciones), el
dictamen en mayoria de la Proposicion Legislativa 00098-2026-2031-CD, que delega
al Poder Ejecutivo la facultad de legislar en materia de seguridad ciudadana y
del fenomeno El Nino por un plazo de noventa (90) dias calendario."

The full sequence of one sitting explains every apparently contradictory report,
and all three tallies are consistent rather than in conflict: the proposal was
FIRST REJECTED (49-73-2); then BOTH minority dictamenes, Fuerza Popular's and
Juntos por el Peru's, were DISAPPROVED -- so Alegria's 20-points-plus-nine text
was voted down and is moot, and the article-78 publication gate never had to be
cleared; then the majority dictamen was re-voted on a reconsideracion moved by
Roxana Rocha Gallegos, which itself carried 75-33-16, and the text was approved
66-34-24. It now goes to the SENATE under arts. 116(h) and 148.

The approved scope is citizen security and El Nino ONLY -- the same narrow
formula as the commission text read here on 1 October, whose ten pages returned
zero for comercio exterior, facilitacion del comercio, forzos, arancel, Mincetur
and tributar. I ran the term sweep on the Congreso page with controls reported:
comercio exterior 0, aduan 0, arancel 0, forzos 0, facilitaci 0, tribut 0, with
controls "reconsider" 2 and "Senado" 3 matching. The single "Mincetur" hit was
checked and is a SIDEBAR RELATED-NEWS TEASER ABOUT MACHU PICCHU, not the article.
So the customs bloc is not in the text that passed, and "regimen tributario" --
its nearest surviving relative -- died with the Fuerza Popular text.

THE CONCLUSION IS STILL HEDGED, DELIBERATELY. The approved text itself has not
been read, so whether the Pleno amended the committee formula is unconfirmed,
and the Senate has not acted. The 1 October "route closed" conclusion was voided
on 6 October by the rejection; today's outcome largely restores its substance by
a different path, but it is NOT reinstated -- a narrow delegation that has passed
one chamber unread is not a closed route. Peru stays Partial; the July bill is
still tabled and nothing is enacted.

(8 Oct, EU CBAM comments); the newest forced-labour-SPECIFIC item is still
2026-17926 of 2 September. THE PROTOCOL GAP FIX FROM 6 OCTOBER HAS NOW PAID
FOR ITSELF: the agency filter does not return 20650 at all.

ROUTE NOTE: federalregister.gov's full_text .txt endpoint 302-redirects to
unblock.federalregister.gov and serves a 10,596-byte access wall AS HTTP 200.
Two batches were misled by it; one inferred "zero forced-labor hits" from the
wall itself. The API `excerpts` field and the govinfo PDF are the reliable
routes. Another 200-is-not-a-read, caught by a control word.

EL SALVADOR: THE FIRST DOWNGRADE IN THIS FILE, AND IT CAME FROM READING A TEXT
THAT HAD NEVER BEEN READ. Yesterday's entry flagged that USTR's 15 September
list of twelve OMITS El Salvador although Article 27-A has been in force since
March 2026, inside USTR's own window. That flag was the thread worth pulling.

Route note first, because it is why this took weeks: the URL in column 6 is a
DOWNLOAD LANDING PAGE, not the PDF, and the file arrives with EIGHT JUNK BYTES
ahead of the %PDF header, which must be stripped before any parser opens it.
Stripped: 178,137 bytes, 20 pages, 54,925 characters, control "aduana" 84.

I read Article 27-A myself rather than take the recommendation. Its heading is
"CONTROL ADUANERO EN MATERIA DE TRABAJO FORZOSO" -- control, not prohibition.
It requires importers to file a sworn declaration that goods were not made with
forced, compulsory or child labour; lets the DGA demand more documentation on
reasonable doubt; and provides that FALSITY IN THE DECLARATION OR FAILURE TO
PRODUCE INFORMATION may bring suspension, preventive retention, "LA DENEGATORIA
DEL DESPACHO" or other customs measures.

THERE IS NO SENTENCE PROHIBITING THE IMPORTATION OF FORCED-LABOUR GOODS, and no
power to prohibit. The denial-of-clearance power is triggered by a DOCUMENTARY
failure, not by a finding that the goods were made with forced labour. That is
a sworn-disclosure regime with customs enforcement, which is a different
instrument from an import ban.

This file's own comparisons decide it. Costa Rica's Article 12 gives only
monitoring and is Partial(c). India's Para 2.20B says "The import of goods
produced or manufactured, wholly or in part, through the use of forced labour
is prohibited" and The Bahamas's s.208A says "The Minister may, by Order,
prohibit the importation" -- both Yes, both containing an express prohibition
or power to prohibit. El Salvador has NEITHER. It sits above Costa Rica
(it can deny clearance) and below India, Trinidad and The Bahamas (it never
prohibits importation). "An incomplete regime that falls short of a full import
prohibition" is limb (c) almost verbatim.

THE COUNTER-ARGUMENT IS RECORDED, NOT SUPPRESSED: an importer whose goods ARE
made with forced labour cannot truthfully declare, so in practice the regime
should exclude them. That is an argument from practical effect, not from the
instrument's terms, and this file's policy is textual -- the same reasoning was
applied against Costa Rica's purpose clause, and against inference generally
after the Bahamas episode. A future run may revisit it; the quoted text is in
the row so that it can.

The row does NOT fall to No on any reading: El Salvador signed the ART on
29 January 2026, an independent limb (b) basis. USTR's omission now has a
MECHANISM rather than being an unexplained gap. What would move it back to Yes
is publication of DGA/MTPS lineamientos containing an actual prohibition.

OTHER SUBSTANTIVE RESULTS.

The render-and-read method unlocked a THIRD document: BANGLADESH's gazette
(723,681 bytes, 88 pages). Its legacy-font text layer is mojibake -- which is
what voided the eleven-zero Bengali sweep -- but a visual read does not depend
on byte mapping at all. Appendix 1 was located by searching Latin chemical
names that survive the mangling, then pages 70-71 were rendered and read:
header "Bangladesh Gazette, Extraordinary, August 24, 2026", items (1) to (28)
ending at gillnets, NO forced-labour item. This confirms the 5 October
enumeration at document level. Limit stated: the 88-page BODY is unread, so a
clause elsewhere is not excluded.

MOROCCO delivered a sharper and more embarrassing version of the same lesson.
Yesterday the five circular PDFs were logged as a tooling failure. Today
pypdfium2 read them DIRECTLY -- they had a text layer all along. No rendering,
no OCR needed. Before concluding a PDF is unreadable, TRY THE OTHER LIBRARY.
All five are clean; the one with the suspicious title "Restrictions
quantitatives a l'importation" turns out to be A GARLIC IMPORT LICENCE.

BAHRAIN: I CORRECT MYSELF. Yesterday I recorded four Arabic terms returning
HTTP 302 as "a tooling failure, not a zero". A batch reconstructed the POST
from the site's own main1.js and tested it with TWO NONSENSE ARABIC STRINGS
that cannot match anything; those return the IDENTICAL 302, while real terms
return 120-140 KB pages. On that evidence the 302 IS the zero-hit signal and
the terms did execute. I tried to reproduce the POST independently and MY OWN
ATTEMPT WAS VOID -- every term including the real controls returned an
identical 404 at 1,245 bytes, so my guessed payload never executed. The
correction therefore rests on the batch's reconstruction, not on my
replication, and is recorded that way. Bahrain's negative is BETTER than
yesterday's entry claimed.

THE EU ENTRY FROM YESTERDAY IS ALSO CORRECTED. I carried that the Commission's
risk database "MISSED its 14 June 2026 deadline". The DEADLINE is now verified
in German statutory text via a commercial mirror ("...bis zum 14. Juni 2026..."),
which also discharges part of the long-outstanding French/German requirement.
THE MISS IS NOT VERIFIED and three sources actively conflict: the contractor
Ecorys says its project runs December 2025 to June 2030; one German firm says
the database WAS built in June; another says it was still to be finalised. So
"missed" is withdrawn. The 20 October textiles webinar is also withdrawn -- a
German search found no such event. New access signature: EUR-Lex,
data.europa.eu and publications.europa.eu all serve an AWS WAF CHALLENGE as
HTTP 202 with awsWafCookieDomainList, distinct from Cloudflare, which WebFetch
also could not penetrate.

JORDAN: lob.gov.jo was FETCHED FOR THE FIRST TIME in this project. It is a wall
but a named one -- HTTP 200 carrying "bobcmn" and a support ID, the F5 BIG-IP
origin-WAF JS challenge served with a success status. Usefully it is the same
signature seen on 14 September but NOT the Angular shell of 15-16 September, so
the challenge ROTATES SHAPES on this host. The ART was re-read at source,
confirming Article 2.9's prohibition commitment and, newly, ARTICLE 6.6(2)'s
entry-into-force mechanism (60 days after mutual written notification) -- so
the clock has a defined start not shown to have begun. Annex I is still not
retrieved; the White House page carries annex HEADINGS ONLY.

COLOMBIA: a new route was found and the negative hardens into something better
than "unreachable". dapre.presidencia.gov.co IS reachable where the rest of the
Colombian estate is not, and serves Decreto 1444 directly (2,191,640 bytes,
"forzos" 31). It carries NO edition stamp: "Diario Oficial" appears exactly
twice, both in body text. With MinCIT showing it zero times and the Gestor
mirror's publication fields still empty, the honest conclusion is no longer
"we cannot reach the source" but that THE DECREE AS PUBLISHED BY THREE ROUTES
SIMPLY DOES NOT CARRY AN EDITION NUMBER.

=== 2026-10-09 03:45 UTC (ADDENDUM 1) ===

Batch 5 applied. ALL SIX BATCHES IN. No further classification changes; final
tally 16 Yes / 15 Partial / 29 No.

PERU -- THE APPROVED TEXT IS READ, AND A ROUTE NOBODY HAD CONSIDERED IS NOW THE
MAIN STORY. Three things landed and the third reframes the row.

FIRST, the approved text was obtained and read, which had never been done. It
is a PURE SCAN, 15 pages, zero text-layer characters -- so it was only readable
because of the method that became available on 8 October. I rendered and read
page 1 myself. Article 1: "...por el plazo de noventa (90) dias calendario...
EXCLUSIVAMENTE en materia de seguridad ciudadana y del Fenomeno El Nino."
That word EXCLUSIVAMENTE is decisive: an express exclusivity limit on the face
of the text, not an inference from a topic list. Article 2 runs 2.1.1-2.1.6 and
2.2.1-2.2.14; the only trade-adjacent words in the document are "importacion"
of FIREARMS and SUNAT as a participating entity. The delegation route via this
law is closed ON THE TEXT -- a far stronger statement than the five-run
inference that was built up and then voided on 6 October.

SECOND, the Senate has not acted and today's silence carries no information.
The expediente shows the text remitted to the Senate at 7 Oct 18:01 Lima. The
Senate's Comision de Constitucion sits at 10:00 a.m. on 9 October, which is
15:00 UTC -- AFTER this run fired at 02:35 UTC. The sixth consecutive timezone
artefact on this row. A claim that Senate rules arts. 25/142 bar senators from
adding matters was NOT verified and is not adopted; if wrong, a Senate
amendment is the one way the first finding could be undone.

THIRD, AND THE REAL FIND: THE COMMITTEE'S OWN DICTAMEN SAYS A FORCED-LABOUR
IMPORT BAN DOES NOT NEED A LAW AT ALL. I read the relevant section of the
177-page majority dictamen in raw text (428,603 characters, control "dictamen"
188). Section 6.6.3 states the Executive's request item f.3 verbatim as
"Regular la prevencion y erradicacion del ingreso al territorio nacional de
mercancias elaboradas total o parcialmente mediante trabajo forzoso, incluyendo
la prohibicion de su importacion" -- so the ban WAS expressly among the 66
requested faculties, which this file had inferred but never seen stated. The
committee rejected it on Article 104 specificity and unmotivated urgency, and
then added: "...EN TANTO SE DERIVA DEL [CPTPP], aprobado... mediante la
Resolucion Legislativa N 31286 el 16 de julio de 2021 y... ratificado mediante
el Decreto Supremo N 042-2021-RE... EN ESTRICTO NO REQUIERE DE UNA LEY. PUEDE
REGULARSE MEDIANTE DECRETO SUPREMO en tanto los Tratados suscritos con arreglo
a la Constitucion forman parte del derecho nacional, de conformidad con el
articulo 55 de la Constitucion."

The congressional committee is saying Peru could impose the ban BY EXECUTIVE
DECREE, without Congress, because CPTPP is already domestic law under Article
55. On that view the ten days this file spent tracking the delegation fight
were largely beside the point. THE THING TO WATCH IS NOW EL PERUANO FOR A
DECRETO SUPREMO, not the Senate. No such decree was found, but that rests on
one search and El Peruano was NOT checked -- a thin negative and the first task
for the next run. Section 6.6.2 confirms the adjacent customs bloc was rejected
on the same grounds.

FOURTH, the July bill is finally identified BY NUMBER and carries a caveat that
goes to this row's own basis: PL 14852/2025-PE, an Executive bill presented
2 July 2026, "EN COMISION" since 6 July -- but sitting in the 2021-2026
CONGRESS, and WHETHER IT LAPSED IS UNVERIFIED. Limb (a) of Peru's Partial rests
on a formally tabled bill, so if it lapsed the classification needs
re-examination. Current-period register searches return zero for "trabajo
forzoso" with working controls ("facultades" 3, "aduanas" 4), which is
consistent with lapse but does not establish it. Second task for next run.

PHILIPPINES -- the inconsistency flagged yesterday is RESOLVED, and BOTH
competing dates were wrong. The JAO was signed 23 JULY, the SAME DAY as the
determination. BusinessWorld with datePublished 2026-07-23T13:03:45Z says
"signed by the department on Thursday", and 23 July 2026 is a Thursday by
calendar check. This row's "24 July" was almost certainly a publication date
read as a signing date. What it does NOT settle: which came first within the
day, so "in anticipation" versus "in response" stays open.

RUSSIA -- yesterday's void is resolved and the negative restored, with better
controls. The API worked on retry, confirming intermittency rather than loss,
and the control that failed yesterday passed at exactly its 7 October value
(511). Two further controls were added and pass (1,688 and 5). All
forced-labour formulations zero; "import ban" returns 4 and "import of goods"
2 on unrelated titles, which usefully shows the register does surface
import instruments when they exist. Now reproduced on a second day.

SOUTH KOREA -- the three supply-chain bills are read and all are zero, making
NINE bill bodies read at document level across two days. The new gap is stated
precisely: the API reports 48 customs bills, only five are read, so ~43 remain
-- and paging could not be made to work, since pIndex, pSize and PAGE all
returned identical first-five results. That is rule 19 applied to our own loop,
and it is reported as unresolved rather than as a property of the API.

SRI LANKA -- the gazette was re-read at primary source and the operative
sentence is now quoted: "The Importation of any goods that are mined, produced
or manufactured wholly or partly by forced labour is hereby prohibited."
A ROUTE CHANGE MATTERS: documents.gov.lk NOW 404s, so this row's citation no
longer resolves; imexport.gov.lk is the working source. Regulation 4 requires
evidence "as may be prescribed by the Director General of Customs" -- precisely
the missing guideline. The guideline search is now near-exhaustive: the REST
pages list was paginated TO THE END at 104 pages carrying 1,219 PDF links, of
which 26 are dated July-October 2026 and none is a guideline. Only PDFs
reachable from neither pages nor media remain unprobed.

PAKISTAN -- the designation-dependent structure of SRO 704(I)/2026 is now
explicit: the federal government "will periodically identify and notify
specific goods, entities, and countries to which the restriction applies".
That is the same shape as India, Trinidad and The Bahamas, so Pakistan's Yes
rests on the structure USTR itself counts. The ECC lead was chased and
half-resolved: Profit (datePublished verified 2026-07-15) confirms ECC approval
of an ILO-C29 definition, but NO corresponding SRO number was found, so it is
not shown to be notified.

SINGAPORE -- today's check is WEAK and labelled so rather than counted as a
refresh. "forced" returns zero on the MTI newsroom, but the controls "tariff"
and "301" ALSO return zero, so the extraction is not reaching article text and
the zero is uninformative. Void sweep, not a negative. The row stands on the
6 October raw-bytes reading.

Qatar, Saudi Arabia and South Africa were lighter than instructed this pass --

than refreshed.
